**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, followed by nothing else.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies. 
From the given information, we can see that there are several testimonies and evidences that describe the events and setting of the crime scene. We need to find a pair that contradicts each other.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction. 
Proposition 2 states: "If the coffee cup is covered by the table cloth and the table cloth is stained by coffee then the coffee cup is in front of the table." This proposition can be applied to the crime scene.

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction. 
Testimony 3 states: "Then the serving girl in question brought over the javaccino." This implies that the coffee cup was brought to the table, and thus, it should be in front of the table. However, Evidence 13 states: "The coffee contained potassium cyanide. Covered in the victim and Maggey's fingerprints. Facing the cup, one can see a lip stain on the front, and a floral looking handle to the right." This evidence does not directly contradict the testimony, but it provides more information about the coffee cup.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction. 
After analyzing the testimonies and evidences, we can see that Testimony 1 states: "He had a newspaper in his right hand, and the noisy brat kept rustling its pages!" This testimony implies that the victim was holding a newspaper in his right hand. However, Evidence 3 states: "Dates from December 3rd, the day of the incident. On it is a doodle that said 'MC Bomber' with an arrow pointing to '100,000' that was crossed out. 'Ahahahaha' and a laughing face are written next to it. Left by a costumer in Trés Bien on Dec. 3rd. Click here for details. Left by the victim. Doodle is in victim's handwriting." This evidence implies that the victim was doodling on the newspaper, which means he was not holding it in his right hand as stated in the testimony.

**Step 5:** Provide your answer in JSON format. 
{"evidence": 3, "testimony": 1}

This contradicts with Evidence 3, the Defendant's Leather Gloves, which has a bloodstain on only the right glove, suggesting that not both hands were necessarily bloody if we consider the defendant as the assailant.

**Step 5:** Given the analysis, the contradiction lies between the testimonies about both hands being bloody and the evidence showing a bloodstain on only one glove.
